First, I need to identify the type of sequence given by the numbers 7, 49, 343,...

I will check if this is a geometric progression (GP) by calculating the ratio between consecutive terms.

Let's solve the problem step by step.

**Given Sequence:**  
7, 49, 343,...

**Objective:** Find the 12\textsuperscript{th} term of the sequence.

---

### Step 1: Identify the Type of Progression

First, determine whether this is an arithmetic or geometric progression.

- **Arithmetic Progression (AP):** Each term increases by a constant difference.
- **Geometric Progression (GP):** Each term is multiplied by a constant ratio to get the next term.

Let's check:

\[
\frac{49}{7} = 7 \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{343}{49} = 7
\]

Since there's a consistent multiplication factor of **7**, this is a **Geometric Progression (GP)**.

---

### Step 2: Determine the Parameters

For a GP, the general term is given by:

\[
T_n = a \times r^{(n-1)}
\]

Where:
- \( T_n \) = nth term
- \( a \) = first term
- \( r \) = common ratio
- \( n \) = number of terms

From the sequence:
- First term (\( a \)) = 7
- Common ratio (\( r \)) = 7 (as calculated above)

---

### Step 3: Find the 12\textsuperscript{th} Term

We need to find \( T_{12} \):

\[
T_{12} = 7 \times 7^{(12-1)} = 7 \times 7^{11}
\]

Simplify:

\[
7 \times 7^{11} = 7^{1 + 11} = 7^{12}
\]

Calculate \( 7^{12} \):

\[
7^{12} = 13,841,287,201
\]

The problem involves finding the 12th term of a geometric progression where the third term (T₃) is 24 and the fifth term (T₅) is 384.

1. **Set up equations based on given terms:**
   - T₃ = ar² = 24
   - T₅ = ar⁴ = 384

2. **Divide the second equation by the first to find the common ratio (r):**
   \[
   \frac{ar^4}{ar^2} = \frac{384}{24} \\
   r^2 = 16 \\
   r = 4
   \]

3. **Solve for the first term (a) using T₃:**
   \[
   a(4)^2 = 24 \\
   16a = 24 \\
   a = \frac{24}{16} = \frac{3}{2}
   \]

4. **Find the 12th term (T₁₂) using Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹:**
   \[
   T_{12} = a \cdot r^{11} \\
   T_{12} = \frac{3}{2} \cdot 4^{11}
   \]

5. **Calculate 4¹¹ and multiply by (3/2):**
   - \(4^{11} = 4,194,304\)
   - \(T_{12} = \frac{3}{2} \cdot 4,194,304 = 6,291,456\)

**Final Answer:**
The 12th term is **6,291,456**.

Now, let's compute the sum of an infinite geometric series with the first term \( a = 1 \) and common ratio \( r = \frac{2}{3} \).

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the Series Parameters:**
   - First term (\( a \)) = 1
   - Common ratio (\( r \)) = \( \frac{2}{3} \)

2. **Check for Convergence:**
   - Since \( |r| < 1 \), the series converges.

3. **Apply the Sum Formula:**
   - The sum of an infinite geometric series is given by:
     \[
     S_{\infty} = \frac{a}{1 - r}
     \]
   
4. **Substitute the Values:**
   \[
   S_{\infty} = \frac{1}{1 - \frac{2}{3}} = \frac{1}{\frac{1}{3}} = 3
   \]

**Final Answer:**

The sum of the infinite geometric series is:
\[
\boxed{3}
\]

To determine the number of terms required for the sum of the geometric progression \(3, \frac{3}{2}, \frac{3}{4}, \ldots\) to reach \(\frac{6141}{1024}\), we use the formula for the sum of the first \(n\) terms of a geometric series:

\[
S_n = a \cdot \frac{1 - r^n}{1 - r}
\]

where:
- \(a = 3\)
- \(r = \frac{1}{2}\)
- \(S_n = \frac{6141}{1024}\)

\[

\]

\[
1 - (\frac{1}{2})^n = \frac{6141}{6144}
\]
\[
(\frac{1}{2})^n = \frac{3}{6144} = \frac{1}{2048}
\]

Since \(2048 = 2^{11}\):

\[
n = 11
\]

The number of terms required is **11**.

\[ S = \frac{a_1}{1 - r} \]

To find the **Sum of an Infinite Geometric Series** given the first two terms, follow these steps:

### Given:
- First term (\( a_1 \)) = \( \frac{13}{12} \)
- Second term (\( a_2 \)) = \( -\frac{1}{12} \)

### Step 1: Find the Common Ratio (\( r \))
The common ratio \( r \) in a geometric progression is found by dividing the second term by the first term:
\[ r = \frac{a_2}{a_1} = \frac{-\frac{1}{12}}{\frac{13}{12}} = -\frac{1}{13} \]

### Step 2: Use the Formula for the Sum of an Infinite Geometric Series
The formula to calculate the sum \( S \) of an infinite geometric series is:
\[ S = \frac{a_1}{1 - r} \]
Substitute the known values into the formula:
\[
S = \frac{\frac{13}{12}}{1 - \left(-\frac{1}{13}\right)} = \frac{\frac{13}{12}}{1 + \frac{1}{13}} = \frac{\frac{13}{12}}{\frac{14}{13}}
\]

### Step 3: Simplify the Expression
Multiply numerator by the reciprocal of the denominator:
\[
S = \frac{13}{12} \times \frac{13}{14} = \frac{169}{168}
\]

The sum of the infinite geometric series is:

### Solution:
1. **Identify the First and Last Terms:**
   - First term (\(a\)) = 1
   - Last term (\(l\)) = 4096

2. **Determine the Number of Intervals:**
   - We need to insert three numbers between 1 and 4096, making a total of five terms.

   \[
   l = a \times r^{n}
   \]
   
   Where:
   - \(l = 4096\)
   - \(a = 1\)
   - \(n = 5\) (since we have 5 terms in total)

   \[

   r = 2
   \]

5. **Final Sequence:**
   
   The complete sequence in geometric progression is:
   
   \[
   1, \ 2, \ 4, \ 8, \ 4096

The three numbers to be inserted between 1 and 4096 are **2, 4,** and **8**.

Sum: 90 + 810 = 900, which also matches.

The problem involves a geometric progression where the first term is 10, and the sum of the third and fifth terms equals 900. By setting up the equation based on these conditions and solving it, we find that the common ratio can be either 3 or -3.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the given information:**
   - First term (a) = 10
   - Sum of third and fifth terms = 900

2. **Express the third and fifth terms using the common ratio 'r':**
   - Third term = \( a \cdot r^{2} = 10r^{2} \)
   - Fifth term = \( a \cdot r^{4} = 10r^{4} \)

3. **Set up the equation based on the sum:**
   - \( 10r^{2} + 10r^{4} = 900 \)

4. **Factor out common terms and simplify:**
   - Factor 10 from both terms:
     \( 10(r^{2} + r^{4}) = 900 \)
   - Divide both sides by 10:
     \( r^{2} + r^{4} = 90 \)

5. **Substitute to reduce the equation:**
   - Let \( y = r^{2} \), then the equation becomes:
     \( y^{2} + y - 90 = 0 \)

6. **Solve the quadratic equation for 'y':**
   - Using the quadratic formula:
     \( y = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 360}}{2} = \frac{-1 \pm 19}{2} \)
   - Solutions: \( y = 9 \) and \( y = -10 \)

7. **Reject the negative solution since \( y = r^{2} \geq 0 \):**
   - Thus, \( y = 9 \), so:
     \( r^{2} = 9 \)
   - Taking square roots gives two possible solutions:
     \( r = 3 \) or \( r = -3 \)

8. **Verify both values of 'r' in the original condition:**
   - Both values satisfy the equation, confirming they are valid.

**Final Answer:**  
The common ratio can be either 3 or -3.

To determine how much money will be accumulated after **15 years**, we'll use the concept of simple interest.

### Given:
- **Principal (P)**: Rs. 10,000
- **Rate of Interest (R)**: 5% per annum
- **Time Period (T)**: 15 years

### Formula for Simple Interest:
\[ \text{Simple Interest (SI)} = P \times R \times T \]

### Calculation:

\[
\begin{align*}
\text{SI} &= 10,000 \times \frac{5}{100} \times 15 \\
&= 10,000 \times 0.05 \times 15 \\
&= 7,500
\end{align*}
\]

### Total Amount after 15 Years:
\[ \text{Total Amount} = P + \text{SI} = 10,000 + 7,500 = 17,500 \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{17,\!500}
\]

Given that a certain number of workers completed a task in \( n \) days with \( x \) workers each day, we need to determine how many days it took if the number of workers decreased by 4 starting from the third day.

First, we note the following:
- The original work required is \( W = n \times x \).
- With the change in the number of workers, the first two days were completed with \( x \) workers each day.
- Starting from the third day, the number of workers decreased by 4 each day.

We can express the total work done as:
\[ W = 2x + (D - 2)(x - 4) \]
where \( D \) is the total number of days taken with the change in workers.

Since the original plan was to complete the task in \( n \) days, we have:
\[ D = n + 8 \]

Substituting \( n = D - 8 \) into the work equation, we get:
\[ W = 2x + (D - 2)(x - 4) \]
\[ W = (D - 8)x \]

Equating both expressions for \( W \):
\[ 2x + (D - 2)(x - 4) = (D - 8)x \]

Expanding and simplifying:
\[ 2x + D(x - 4) - 2(x - 4) = D x - 8x \]
\[ 2x + Dx - 4D - 2x + 8 = Dx - 8x \]
\[ Dx - 4D + 8 = Dx - 8x \]

Subtracting \( Dx \) from both sides:
\[ -4D + 8 = -8x \]

Multiplying by (-1):
\[ 4D - 8 = 8x \]
\[ D - 2 = 2x \]
\[ x = \frac{D - 2}{2} \]

Given that \( n = D - 8 \) and \( n = 2x - 6 \), we substitute \( x \):
\[ n = 2 \left( \frac{D - 2}{2} \right) - 6 \]
\[ n = (D - 2) - 6 \]
\[ n = D - 8 \]

This confirms the relationship. To find the minimal integer \( D \) such that \( x \) is an integer and positive, we check:
- \( D - 10 > 0 \)
- The smallest even \( D \) greater than 10 is 12.

Thus, substituting \( D = 12 \):
\[ x = \frac{12 - 2}{2} = 5 \]
which is an integer and positive.

Therefore, the task took \(\boxed{12}\) days.

To find the **12th term** of the arithmetic progression (AP): 4, 9, 14, ..., follow these steps:

### Step 1: Identify the First Term and Common Difference
- **First term (a₁):** 4
- **Common difference (d):** 
  \[
  d = 9 - 4 = 5
  \]

### Step 2: Use the Formula for the nth Term of an AP
The formula to find the \(n^{th}\) term (\(a_n\)) of an arithmetic progression is:
\[
a_n = a₁ + (n - 1)d
\]
Plugging in the known values:
- \(a₁ = 4\)
- \(d = 5\)
- \(n = 12\)

### Step 3: Calculate the 12th Term
\[
a_{12} = 4 + (12 - 1) \times 5 = 4 + 11 \times 5 = 4 + 55 = 59
\]

### Final Answer
The **12th term** of the AP is:
\[
\boxed{59}
\]

Here, the number of pots is decreasing by 2 each time—46, 44, 42,… So the first term (a₁) is 46, the common difference (d) is -2 because it's getting smaller.

I need to find out how many terms are there until I reach 4. The last term (aₙ) is given as 4. There's a formula for the nth term of an arithmetic sequence: aₙ = a₁ + (n-1)d. Let me plug in the values I know.

21 = n - 1

Then, add 1 to both sides to get n:

n = 22.

So, there are 22 rows in total. I think that makes sense because starting from 46 and decreasing by 2 each time until reaching 4 would take (46 - 4)/2 + 1 steps. Let me check: (46-4)=42, divided by 2 is 21, plus 1 gives 22. Yep, that checks out.

The number of rows can be determined using the arithmetic progression formula. The first term \( a_1 = 46 \), the common difference \( d = -2 \), and the last term \( a_n = 4 \). Using the formula for the nth term:

\[ a_n = a_1 + (n-1)d \]

Substituting the known values:

\[ 4 = 46 + (n-1)(-2) \]
\[ -42 = (n-1)(-2) \]
\[ n - 1 = 21 \]
\[ n = 22 \]

So, there are 22 rows.

a_58 = 14 + (58 - 1) * 2  
= 14 + 57 * 2  
= 14 + 114

10 = n - 1

The problem involves determining after how many years an annual salary will reach Rs.14,000 given it starts at Rs.10,000 in 1995 and increases by Rs.400 each year.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the Known Values:**
   - Initial salary (a₁) = Rs.10,000
   - Common difference (d) = Rs.400 per year
   - Target salary (aₙ) = Rs.14,000

2. **Use the Arithmetic Progression Formula for nth Term:**
   
   The formula is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   
3. **Plug in the Known Values:**
   
   Substitute \(a_1 = 10,000\), \(d = 400\), and \(a_n = 14,000\) into the formula:
   \[
   14,000 = 10,000 + (n - 1) \times 400
   \]
   
4. **Solve for n:**
   
   Subtract 10,000 from both sides:
   \[
   4,000 = (n - 1) \times 400
   \]
   
   Divide both sides by 400 to find \(n - 1\):
   \[
   \frac{4,000}{400} = n - 1 \\
   10 = n - 1
   \]
   
   Add 1 to both sides:
   \[
   n = 11
   \]
   
5. **Determine the Year:**
   
   Since the salary starts in 1995, adding 10 years (since \(n\) represents the number of terms starting from 1995) brings us to the year:
   \[
   1995 + 10 = 2005
   \]
   
**Final Answer:**

The salary will reach Rs.14,000 in the year **2005**.

To find the sum of the first **44** terms of an arithmetic progression (AP) with a common difference \( D = 14 \) and the \( 44^{th} \) term \( T_{44} = 298 \), we can follow these steps:

### Step 1: Find the First Term (\( a_1 \))
The general formula for the \( n^{th} \) term of an AP is:
\[
T_n = a_1 + (n - 1)D
\]
Given that \( T_{44} = 298 \), we can plug in the known values to find \( a_1 \):
\[
298 = a_1 + (44 - 1) \times 14 \\
298 = a_1 + 43 \times 14 \\
298 = a_1 + 602 \\
a_1 = 298 - 602 \\
a_1 = -304
\]

### Step 2: Calculate the Sum of the First 44 Terms (\( S_{44} \))
The formula for the sum of the first \( n \) terms of an AP is:
\[
S_n = \frac{n}{2} \times (a_1 + a_n)
\]
We already know:
- \( n = 44 \)
- \( a_1 = -304 \)
- \( a_{44} = 298 \)

Plugging these values into the formula:
\[
S_{44} = \frac{44}{2} \times (-304 + 298) \\
S_{44} = 22 \times (-6) \\
S_{44} = -132
\]

***********************<think>
First, I need to understand the problem. The user is referring to an arithmetic progression where the difference between the 34th and the 20th term is 14.

In an arithmetic sequence, each term after the first is obtained by adding a constant called the common difference (d). So, the nth term can be expressed as:
a_n = a + (n - 1)d

Given that the difference between the 34th and 20th terms is 14, I'll set up an equation using their general formulas.

Subtracting the 20th term from the 34th term gives me:
(a + 33d) - (a + 19d) = 14

Simplifying this equation eliminates the first term and leaves me with:
14d = 14

Dividing both sides by 14, I find that d equals 1.
</think>

**Solution:**

We are given an arithmetic progression where the difference between the \(34^{th}\) and \(20^{th}\) terms is 14. We need to find the common difference (\(d\)).

In an arithmetic progression, the \(n^{th}\) term is given by:
\[ a_n = a + (n - 1)d \]
where:
- \(a\) is the first term,
- \(d\) is the common difference,
- \(n\) is the term number.

Given that the difference between the \(34^{th}\) and \(20^{th}\) terms is 14, we can write:
\[
(a + 33d) - (a + 19d) = 14
\]
Simplifying this equation:
\[
( a - a ) + (33d - 19d) = 14 \\
14d = 14 \\
d = \frac{14}{14} \\
d = 1
\]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{1}
\]

The arithmetic mean (AM) of the two numbers A = 10 and B = 8 is calculated as:

\[
AM = \frac{A + B}{2} = \frac{10 + 8}{2} = 9
\]

The geometric mean (GM) is calculated as:

\[

To solve the problem of finding two numbers whose sum is 12 and their geometric mean is √(6 + √35), we can follow these steps:

1. **Define Variables:**
   Let the two numbers be \( x \) and \( y \).

2. **Set Up Equations Based on Given Information:**
   
   - The sum of the two numbers:
     \[
     x + y = 12
     \]
     
   - The geometric mean of the two numbers is given by √(6 + √35). Therefore:
     \[
     \sqrt{xy} = \sqrt{6 + \sqrt{35}}
     \]

3. **Simplify the Geometric Mean Equation:**
   
   Square both sides to eliminate the square root:
   \[
   xy = 6 + \sqrt{35}
   \]

4. **Form a Quadratic Equation:**
   
   Using the sum and product of roots formula for quadratic equations, we have:
   \[
   t^2 - (x + y)t + xy = 0
   \]
   
   Substituting the known values:
   \[
   t^2 - 12t + (6 + \sqrt{35}) = 0
   \]

5. **Solve the Quadratic Equation Using the Quadratic Formula:**
   
   The quadratic formula is:
   \[
   t = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a}
   \]
   
   For our equation, \( a = 1 \), \( b = -12 \), and \( c = 6 + \sqrt{35} \).
   
   Plugging in these values:
   \[
   t = \frac{12 \pm \sqrt{(−12)^2 − 4(1)(6+\sqrt{35})}}{2(1)}
   \]
   \[
   t = \frac{12 \pm \sqrt{144 - 24 - 4\sqrt{35}}}{2}
   \]
   \[
   t = \frac{12 \pm \sqrt{120 - 4\sqrt{35}}}{2}
   \]

6. **Calculate the Discriminant:**
   
   The discriminant is \( 120 - 4\sqrt{35} \).
   
   Let's approximate its value:
   \[
   \sqrt{35} \approx 5.916
   \]
   \[
   120 - 4(5.916) = 120 - 23.664 = 96.336
   \]
   
   So, the discriminant \( \sqrt{96.336} \approx 9.816 \).

7. **Find the Roots:**
   
   Plugging back into the formula:
   \[
   t = \frac{12 \pm 9.816}{2}
   \]
   
   This gives two solutions:
   \[
   t_1 = \frac{12 + 9.816}{2} = \frac{21.816}{2} \approx 10.908
   \]
   \[
   t_2 = \frac{12 - 9.816}{2} = \frac{2.184}{2} \approx 1.092
   \]

8. **Final Answer:**
   
   The two numbers are approximately:
   \[
   \boxed{10.91 \text{ and } 1.09}
   \]

To calculate the **Geometric Mean** (ہندسی مطلب) for the numbers \(12\), \(16\), \(32\), and \(54\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand Geometric Mean
The geometric mean of a set of \(n\) positive real numbers is defined as the nth root of the product of all the numbers.

\[
\text{Geometric Mean} = \sqrt[n]{x_1 \times x_2 \times \dots \times x_n}
\]

### Step 2: Calculate the Product
Multiply all the given numbers together:

\[
12 \times 16 \times 32 \times 54
\]

Let's compute this step by step:
- \(12 \times 16 = 192\)
- \(192 \times 32 = 6,144\)
- \(6,144 \times 54 = 329,856\)

### Step 3: Find the Fourth Root
Since there are four numbers (\(n=4\)), we take the fourth root of the product:

\[
\sqrt[4]{329,\!856}
\]

Using a calculator or estimation methods:
- The fourth root of \(329,856\) is approximately \(20.17\).

### Final Answer
The geometric mean (ہندسی مطلب) for the numbers \(12\), \(16\), \(32\), and \(54\) is:

\[
\boxed{20.17}
\]

**Solution:**

We are given the annual percentage growth rates for a business from 2000 to 2005 as follows:

- **2000:** 50%
- **2001:** 72%
- **2002:** 54%
- **2003:** 82%
- **2004:** 93%

To find the **Geometric Mean** of these growth rates, follow these steps:

1. **Convert Percentage Growth Rates to Decimal Form:**
   
   \[
   0.50,\ 0.72,\ 0.54,\ 0.82,\ 0.93
   \]

2. **Calculate the Geometric Mean:**

   The geometric mean (\(GM\)) is calculated using the formula:

   \[
   GM = \left(\prod_{i=1}^{n} x_i\right)^{\frac{1}{n}}
   \]

   Where:
   
   - \(x_i\) are the individual growth rates
   - \(n\) is the number of growth rates

   Plugging in the values:

   \[
   GM = \sqrt[5]{0.50 \times 0.72 \times 0.54 \times 0.82 \times 0.93}
   \]

   First, calculate the product of the growth rates:

   \[
   0.50 \times 0.72 = 0.36
   \]
   
   \[
   0.36 \times 0.54 = 0.1944
   \]
   
   \[
   0.1944 \times 0.82 = 0.159408
   \]
   
   \[
   0.159408 \times 0.93 ≈ 0.1473
   \]

   Now, find the fifth root of this product:

   \[
   GM = \sqrt[5]{0.1473} ≈ 0.62\% 
   \]

3. **Convert Back to Percentage:**

   To express the geometric mean as a percentage:

   \[
   GM ≈ 0.62
   \]
   
   (Since we're dealing with growth rates, it's typical to present this as 0.62 or 0.62%.)

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{0.62}

The expected number of groups where two people share consecutive birthdays among \( N \) individuals can be calculated by considering each pair. For any given pair, there are 2 possible consecutive birthday pairs (e.g., January 1 and January 2). Since the year has 365 days, the probability that a specific pair has consecutive birthdays is \( \frac{2}{365} \).

The total number of pairs in \( N \) individuals is given by the combination formula:
\[
C(N, 2) = \frac{N(N - 1)}{2}
\]

Using linearity of expectation, the expected value \( E[S] \) (where \( S \) represents the number of consecutive birthday pairs) is calculated as:
\[
E[S] = C(N, 2) \times P(\text{consecutive}) = \frac{N(N - 1)}{2} \times \frac{2}{365}
\]
Simplifying this expression gives:
\[
E[S] = \frac{N(N - 1)}{365}
\]

Therefore, the expected number of consecutive birthday pairs among \( N \) individuals is:

$$
\boxed{\dfrac{N (N-1)}{365}}
$$

First, I recognize that there are 14 numbers whose arithmetic mean (AM) is given as 6. 

The arithmetic mean formula tells me that:
AM = Sum of all numbers / Total count

Using this, I can calculate the total sum by multiplying the AM by the number of terms:
Sum = 14 × 6 = 84

Next, if we add three more numbers to these 14 numbers, the new total count becomes 17.

The new mean (AM') is calculated as:
AM' = New Sum / Total new count
AM' = 84 + sum of added numbers / 17

**Solution:**

We are given:
- Total sum (\( S \)) = 38
- Number of values (\( n \)) = 40
- Incorrect geometric mean mentioned = 36

Our goal is to find the correct arithmetic mean (AM) and geometric mean (GM).

1. **Calculate the Arithmetic Mean:**

   The arithmetic mean \( AM \) is calculated as:
   \[
   AM = \frac{S}{n} = \frac{38}{40} = 0.95
   \]

2. **Calculate the Geometric Mean:**

   The geometric mean \( GM \) is given by:
   \[
   GM = \sqrt[n]{\prod_{i=1}^{n} a_i}
   \]
   
   However, since we don't have the individual values but are given that the product of all numbers is 276,400,000,000,000, we can compute \( GM \) as:
   \[
   GM = (276,\!400,\!000,\!000,\!000)^{1/40}

   \]

To find the 12th term of the geometric progression with terms **7**, **49**, **343**, ..., we can follow these steps:

### Step-by-Step Explanation:

1. **Identify the Common Ratio (r):**
   - The given sequence is a geometric progression where each term is multiplied by a constant ratio to get the next term.
   - Calculate \( r \) using the first two terms:
     \[
     r = \frac{49}{7} = 7
     \]
   
2. **Use the Formula for the nth Term of a Geometric Progression:**
   The formula is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 \times r^{(n-1)}
   \]
   Where:
   - \( a_1 \) is the first term,
   - \( r \) is the common ratio,
   - \( n \) is the term number.

3. **Plug in the Values:**
   Here, \( a_1 = 7 \), \( r = 7 \), and \( n = 12 \).
   \[
   a_{12} = 7 \times 7^{(12-1)} = 7 \times 7^{11}
   \]

4. **Calculate \( 7^{11} \):**
   - \( 7^1 = 7 \)
   - \( 7^2 = 49 \)
   - \( 7^3 = 343 \)
   - \( 7^4 = 2,\!401 \)
   - \( 7^5 = 16,\!807 \)
   - \( 7^6 = 117,\!649 \)
   - \( 7^7 = 823,\!543 \)
   - \( 7^8 = 5,\!764,\!801 \)
   - \( 7^9 = 40,\!353,\!607 \)
   - \( 7^{10} = 282,\!475,\!249 \)
   - \( 7^{11} = 1,\!977,\!326,\!743 \)

5. **Compute the Final Value:**
   \[
   a_{12} = 7 \times 1,\!977,\!326,\!743 = 13,\!841,\!287,\!201
   \]

### Final Answer:
\[

\]

To solve the problem using geometric progression principles, let's analyze the given sequence: 4, 64, 1024, ..., 262144.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Identify the First Term (\(a\)) and Common Ratio (\(r\)):**
   - The first term \(a\) is clearly 4.
   - To find the common ratio \(r\), divide the second term by the first term:
     \[
     r = \frac{64}{4} = 16
     \]

2. **Determine the Number of Terms (\(n\)):**
   - We need to find which term corresponds to 262,144.
   - Use the formula for the \(n\)th term of a geometric sequence:
     \[
     T_n = a \times r^{(n-1)}
     \]
   - Plug in the known values and solve for \(n\):
     \[
     262144 = 4 \times 16^{(n-1)}
     \]
     \[
     \frac{262144}{4} = 16^{(n-1)} \\
     65536 = 16^{(n-1)}
     \]
   - Recognize that \(16^4 = 65536\), so:
     \[
     n - 1 = 4 \\
     n = 5
     \]

### Final Answer:

The term 262,144 is the **5th** term in the geometric progression.

The problem is about a geometric progression where the third term is 24 and the fifth term is 384. I need to find the twelfth term.

First, recall that in a geometric sequence, each term after the first is found by multiplying the previous term by a constant called the common ratio (r). The general formula for the nth term of a geometric progression is:

aₙ = a₁ * r^(n-1)

Given:
Third term (a₃) = 24
Fifth term (a₅) = 384

Set up equations using the formula:
a₃ = a₁ * r² = 24
a₅ = a₁ * r⁴ = 384

Divide the fifth term equation by the third term equation to eliminate a₁:

(a₁ * r⁴) / (a₁ * r²) = 384 / 24
r² = 16

Take the square root of both sides to find r:
r = √16
r = 4 or -4

Now, substitute r back into one of the equations to solve for a₁. Using the third term equation:

a₃ = a₁ * r² = 24
If r = 4: a₁ * 16 = 24 => a₁ = 24 / 16 = 3/2 or 1.5

If r = -4: a₁ * (-4)² = 24 => a₁ * 16 = 24 => a₁ = 24 / 16 = 3/2 or 1.5

So, regardless of whether r is positive or negative, a₁ is 3/2.

Now, to find the twelfth term (a₁₂):

a₁₂ = a₁ * r^(12-1) = a₁ * r¹¹

Since we have two possible values for r (+4 and -4), I need to calculate both possibilities.

If r = 4:
a₁₂ = 3/2 * 4^11
Calculate 4^11: 4^10 is 1,048,576; so 4^11 is 4,194,304.
Then a₁₂ = (3/2) * 4,194,304 = (3) * 2,097,152 = 6,291,456

If r = -4:
a₁₂ = 3/2 * (-4)^11
Since the exponent is odd, (-4)^11 will be negative. Calculate 4^11 as before: 4,194,304.
So a₁₂ = (3/2) * (-4,194,304) = -6,291,456

Therefore, the twelfth term can be either 6,291,456 or -6,291,456 depending on whether r is positive or negative.
</think>

To find the twelfth term of the geometric progression where the third term is 24 and the fifth term is 384:

1. **Identify the terms in the geometric sequence:**
   - Third term (a₃) = a₁ * r² = 24
   - Fifth term (a₅) = a₁ * r⁴ = 384

2. **Find the common ratio (r):**
   - Divide the fifth term by the third term:
     \[
     \frac{a_5}{a_3} = \frac{a_1 r^4}{a_1 r^2} = r^2
     \]
   - \( \frac{384}{24} = 16 = r^2 \)
   - Therefore, \( r = \sqrt{16} = 4 \) or \( r = -4 \)

3. **Find the first term (a₁):**
   - Using a₃: 
     \[
     a_3 = a_1 * r^2 \\
     24 = a_1 * 16 \\
     a_1 = \frac{24}{16} = \frac{3}{2}
     \]

4. **Calculate the twelfth term (a₁₂):**
   - For \( r = 4 \):
     \[
     a_{12} = a_1 * r^{11} \\
     a_{12} = \frac{3}{2} * 4^{11} \\
     4^{11} = 4,194,304 \\
     a_{12} = \frac{3}{2} * 4,194,304 = 6,291,456
     \]
   - For \( r = -4 \):
     \[
     a_{12} = a_1 * (-r)^{11} \\
     Since the exponent is odd, this becomes negative:
     a_{12} = \frac{3}{2} * (-4)^{11} \\
     (-4)^{11} = -4,194,304 \\
     a_{12} = \frac{3}{2} * (-4,194,304) = -6,291,456
     \]

**Final Answer:** The twelfth term is either 6,291,456 or -6,291,456 depending on the value of the common ratio.

\[

\]

\[

To determine the common ratio \( r \) of a geometric progression (GP) with the first term \( a = \frac{13}{12} \) and an infinite sum \( S_{\infty} = -1 \), we use the formula for the sum of an infinite GP:

\[ S_{\infty} = \frac{a}{1 - r} \]

Plugging in the known values:

\[ -1 = \frac{\frac{13}{12}}{1 - r} \]

Solving for \( 1 - r \):

\[ 1 - r = -\frac{12}{13} \]
\[ r = 1 + \frac{12}{13} \]
\[ r = \frac{25}{13} \approx 1.923 \]

However, for an infinite GP to converge, \( |r| < 1 \) must hold. Here, \( r > 1 \), so the series diverges and doesn't have a finite sum.

Answer: The common ratio is \( \boxed{\dfrac{25}{13}} \).

The problem involves calculating the number of ancestors over ten generations, where each generation doubles in number from the previous one (parents, grandparents, great-grandparents, etc.). This forms a geometric progression with the first term \( a = 2 \) and common ratio \( r = 2 \).

To find the total number of ancestors during ten generations, we use the formula for the sum of a geometric series:

\[
S_n = a_1 \times \frac{r^n - 1}{r - 1}
\]

Where:
- \( a_1 = 2 \) (the first term),
- \( r = 2 \) (the common ratio),
- \( n = 10 \) (number of generations).

Plugging in the values:

\[
S_{10} = 2 \times \frac{2^{10} - 1}{2 - 1} = 2 \times 1023 = 2046
\]

Answer: {Final Answer: "2046"}

To find the 14th term of the geometric progression (HP), let's use the given information:

**Given:**
- The 10th term (\(a_{10}\)) = 384
- Common ratio (\(r\)) = 4

**Step-by-Step Solution:**

1. **Understand the Formula for Geometric Progression (GP):**
   
   In a geometric progression, each term is found using the formula:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 \times r^{(n-1)}
   \]
   where:
   - \(a_n\) = nth term
   - \(a_1\) = first term
   - \(r\) = common ratio
   - \(n\) = term number

2. **Find the First Term (\(a_1\)):**
   
   Given that \(a_{10} = 384\) and \(r = 4\), we can set up the equation:
   \[
   a_{10} = a_1 \times r^{9} \\
   384 = a_1 \times 4^{9}
   \]
   
   Solving for \(a_1\):
   \[
   a_1 = \frac{384}{4^{9}} = \frac{384}{262144} = \frac{3}{2048}
   \]

3. **Find the 14th Term (\(a_{14}\)):**
   
   Now, using \(a_1 = \frac{3}{2048}\) and \(r = 4\), we can find \(a_{14}\):
   \[
   a_{14} = a_1 \times r^{13} \\
   a_{14} = \frac{3}{2048} \times 4^{13}
   \]
   
   Calculate \(4^{13}\):
   \[
   4^{13} = (2^2)^{13} = 2^{26} = 67,108,864
   \]
   
   Now, multiply:
   \[
   a_{14} = \frac{3}{2048} \times 67,108,864 \\
   a_{14} = 3 \times 32,768 \\
   a_{14} = 98,304
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{98,\!304}
\]

\( 1 = -1000r^{15} \)

To determine the ninth term of the geometric progression (GP), let's analyze the given information step by step:

**Given:**
- The first term (\(a_1\)) is **-10**.
- The sixth term (\(a_6\)) is equal to its fourth power. 

**Step 1: Understand the GP Formula**

The \(n^{th}\) term of a GP is given by:
\[ a_n = a_1 \times r^{n-1} \]
where \(r\) is the common ratio.

**Step 2: Express the Sixth Term**

Using the formula for the sixth term (\(a_6\)):
\[ a_6 = a_1 \times r^{5} = -10 \times r^5 \]

According to the problem, this term satisfies:
\[ a_6 = (a_6)^4 \]
Substituting the expression for \(a_6\):
\[ -10 \times r^5 = (-10 \times r^5)^4 \]

**Step 3: Simplify the Equation**

Calculate the right-hand side:
\[ (-10r^5)^4 = (-10)^4 \times (r^5)^4 = 10,000 \times r^{20} \]

So, the equation becomes:
\[ -10r^5 = 10,000r^{20} \]
Divide both sides by \( -10r^5 \) (assuming \( r \neq 0 \)):
\[ 1 = -1000r^{15} \]

Solving for \( r \):
\[ r^{15} = -\frac{1}{1000} \]
Taking the fifteenth root:
\[ r = -\left(\frac{1}{1000}\right)^{\frac{1}{15}} = -\sqrt[15]{\frac{1}{1000}} \]

Simplifying further, since \( 1000 = 10^3 \):
\[ r = -\left(10^{-3}\right)^{\frac{1}{15}} = -10^{-\frac{1}{5}} \approx -0.6687 \]

**Step 4: Find the Ninth Term (\(a_9\))**

Using the GP formula for \(a_9\):
\[ a_9 = a_1 \times r^{8} = -10 \times (-0.6687)^{8} \]

Calculating \( (-0.6687)^8 \) gives approximately **0.25**:
\[ a_9 \approx -10 \times 0.25 = -2.5 \]

**Final Answer:**

The ninth term of the geometric progression is:

\boxed{-2.5}

The problem asks for the number of terms \( n \) in a geometric series where the first term is 1, the common ratio is 4, and the sum of the series is 63. 

To find \( n \), we use the formula for the sum of the first \( n \) terms of a geometric series:

\[
S_n = \frac{a(r^n - 1)}{r - 1}
\]

Plugging in \( a = 1 \), \( r = 4 \), and \( S_n = 63 \):

\[
\frac{4^n - 1}{4 - 1} = 63
\]
\[
\frac{4^n - 1}{3} = 63
\]
\[
4^n - 1 = 189
\]
\[
4^n = 190
\]

Taking the logarithm base 4 of both sides:

\[
n = \log_4(190)
\]

Calculating this gives approximately \( n \approx 3.789 \). Since \( n \) must be an integer, there is no solution for which the sum equals exactly 63 with a first term of 1 and common ratio of 4.

**Answer:** There is no positive integer value of \( n \) that satisfies these conditions.

The problem involves finding the common ratio of a geometric progression where the first term is 10 and the sum of the third and fifth terms is 900.

**Solution:**

Let’s denote:
- First term, \( a = 10 \)
- Common ratio, \( r \)

The nth term of a geometric progression is given by:
\[ a_n = a \times r^{n-1} \]

Therefore,
- Third term (\( n=3 \)): \( a_3 = 10r^2 \)
- Fifth term (\( n=5 \)): \( a_5 = 10r^4 \)

Given that their sum is 900:
\[ a_3 + a_5 = 900 \]
Substituting the expressions for \( a_3 \) and \( a_5 \):
\[ 10r^2 + 10r^4 = 900 \]

Factor out 10:
\[ 10(r^2 + r^4) = 900 \]
Divide both sides by 10:
\[ r^2 + r^4 = 90 \]

Let \( y = r^2 \), then the equation becomes:
\[ y^2 + y - 90 = 0 \]

Solving this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula:
\[ y = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a} \]
Here, \( a=1 \), \( b=1 \), and \( c=-90 \):
\[ y = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 360}}{2} = \frac{-1 \pm 19}{2} \]

This yields two solutions:
- \( y = \frac{18}{2} = 9 \)
- \( y = \frac{-20}{2} = -10 \) (Not possible since \( r^2 \) cannot be negative)

Thus, \( y = 9 \), so:
\[ r^2 = 9 \]
Taking the square root:
\[ r = 3 \text{ or } r = -3 \]

**Answer:**
The common ratio is either 3 or -3.

- Annual interest rate (Rate) = 24%

Then, I'll determine the selling price using the formula:
Selling Price (SP) = CP × (1 + Rate/100)

**Solution:**

We are given:
- **Marked Price (MP)** = ₹24000
- **Amount paid in cash** = ₹12000
- **Overheads charges** = ₹1000
- **Annual interest rate (Rate)** = 24%

To find the final amount to be paid, we follow these steps:

1. **Calculate Total Cost Price (CP):**
   \[
   \text{Total CP} = \text{Amount paid in cash} + \text{Overheads charges}
   \]
   \[
   \text{Total CP} = ₹12000 + ₹1000 = ₹13000
   \]

2. **Calculate Selling Price (SP) using the formula:**
   \[
   \text{SP} = \text{CP} × \left(1 + \frac{\text{Rate}}{100}\right)
   \]
   \[
   \text{SP} = ₹13000 × \left(1 + \frac{24}{100}\right) = ₹16120
   \]

Since the **Marked Price (MP)** is equal to the **Selling Price (SP)** in this scenario, we have:

\[
\text{Final Answer:} \boxed{₹16120}
\]

Alright, let's try to figure out how to solve this problem. So, Shمشاد علی is buying a scooter that costs Rs.44,000. He pays Rs.8,000 in cash and then has to pay the remaining amount over 20 years with an annual interest rate of 20%. Hmm, okay.

Now, this seems like an installment loan scenario where he's paying back the balance plus interest each year for 20 years at a 20% annual rate. I remember that in such cases, the amount paid each year would include both principal and interest. To find out how much he needs to pay each year, we can use the formula for an annuity.

The formula for calculating the annual payment (A) is:
A = P * [i(1 + i)^n] / [(1 + i)^n - 1]

Where:
- P is the principal amount, which is Rs.36,000
- i is the annual interest rate, which is 20% or 0.20
- n is the number of years, which is 20

Let me plug these values into the formula step by step.

First, calculate (1 + i)^n:
(1 + 0.20)^20 = 1.2^20. I don't remember exactly what that is, but I know it's a large number because 1.2 multiplied many times grows exponentially. Maybe around 38. Let me check: actually, 1.2^10 is approximately 6.19, so 1.2^20 would be (1.2^10)^2 ≈ 6.19^2 ≈ 38.34.

So, now calculate the numerator:
i * (1 + i)^n = 0.20 * 38.34 ≈ 7.668

Denominator:

   \[
   A = P \times \frac{i(1 + i)^n}{(1 + i)^n - 1}

To find the twelfth term of the arithmetic progression, I first identify the common difference by subtracting the second term from the first.

Next, I apply the formula for the nth term of an arithmetic sequence, which is \( a_n = a_1 + (n-1)d \).

Substituting the known values into the formula allows me to calculate the twelfth term accurately.
</think>

To find the **twelfth term** in the given arithmetic progression:

### Given Sequence:
4, 9, 14, ...

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Identify the First Term (a₁):**
   - The first term of the sequence is \( a_1 = 4 \).

2. **Determine the Common Difference (d):**
   - Subtract the first term from the second term to find the common difference.
     \[
     d = a_2 - a_1 = 9 - 4 = 5
     \]

3. **Use the Formula for the \( n^{th} \) Term of an Arithmetic Progression:**
   - The formula is:
     \[
     a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
     \]
   - Here, we want to find the twelfth term (\( n = 12 \)).

4. **Calculate the Twelfth Term (\( a_{12} \)):**
   - Substitute \( a_1 = 4 \), \( d = 5 \), and \( n = 12 \) into the formula:
     \[
     a_{12} = 4 + (12 - 1) \times 5
     \]
     \[
     a_{12} = 4 + 11 \times 5
     \]
     \[
     a_{12} = 4 + 55
     \]
     \[
     a_{12} = 59
     \]

### Final Answer:
\boxed{59}

11 - 5 = 6

17 - 11 = 6

23 - 17 = 6

Now, I need to find out if 330 is part of this sequence. The general formula for the nth term of an AP is:

a_n = a + (n - 1)d

Where:
- a_n is the nth term
- a is the first term
- d is the common difference
- n is the term number

We can plug in a = 5, d = 6, and set a_n to 330 to see if there's an integer value of n that satisfies the equation.

So,

330 = 5 + (n - 1) * 6

First, subtract 5 from both sides:

325 = (n - 1) * 6

Now, divide both sides by 6 to solve for (n - 1):

325 / 6 ≈ 54.1667

So,

n - 1 ≈ 54.1667

Therefore,

n ≈ 55.1667

The total future value of the investment after 30 years, considering each annual payment with simple interest, is calculated as follows:

Each year, an initial sum of \$10,000 is invested for a varying number of years (from 30 down to 1). The future value of each such investment is computed using the formula:
\[ FV = P \times (1 + R \times T) \]
where \( P = \$10,000 \), \( R = 16\% \), and \( T \) varies from 30 to 1.

The total future value is the sum of these individual future values:
\[ FV_{total} = \sum_{T=1}^{30} P \times (1 + R \times T) \]
This simplifies to:
\[ FV_{total} = nP + PR \times \frac{n(n+1)}{2} \]
where \( n = 30 \).

Substituting the values:
- \( nP = 30 \times \$10,000 = \$300,000 \)
- \( PR = \$10,000 \times 0.16 = \$1,600 \)
- Sum of T from 1 to 30: \( \frac{30 \times 31}{2} = 465 \)

Thus:
\[ FV_{total} = \$300,000 + (\$1,600 \times 465) = \$300,000 + \$744,000 = \$1,044,000 \]

\[
\boxed{1044000}
\]

To determine after how many years Riaz's income will reach Rs. 14,000, we can use the concept of an arithmetic progression since his salary increases by a constant amount each year.

Firstly, identify the given values:
- Initial income (a₁) = Rs. 10,000
- Annual increase (d) = Rs. 400

We are tasked with finding the number of years (n) it will take for his income to reach Rs. 14,000.

The formula for the nth term of an arithmetic progression is:
aₙ = a₁ + (n - 1)d

Substituting the known values into the equation:
14,000 = 10,000 + (n - 1) * 400

Solving for n:
14,000 - 10,000 = (n - 1) * 400
4,000 = (n - 1) * 400
(n - 1) = 4,000 / 400
(n - 1) = 10

Finally:
n = 10 + 1
n = 11

Therefore, it will take Riaz 11 years for his income to reach Rs. 14,000.
</think>

To determine after how many years Riaz's income will reach **Rs. 14,000**, we can use the concept of an arithmetic progression since his salary increases by a constant amount each year.

### Given:
- **Initial income (a₁)** = Rs. **10,000**
- **Annual increase (d)** = Rs. **400**

We need to find the number of years (n) it will take for his income to reach Rs. 14,000.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

The formula for the nth term of an arithmetic progression is:
\[ a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d \]

Substituting the known values into the equation:
\[ 14,000 = 10,000 + (n - 1) \times 400 \]

**Solving for n:**
\[
\begin{align*}
14,000 &= 10,000 + 400(n - 1) \\
14,000 - 10,000 &= 400(n - 1) \\
4,000 &= 400(n - 1) \\
\frac{4,000}{400} &= n - 1 \\
10 &= n - 1 \\
n &= 11
\end{align*}
\]

### Final Answer:
It will take **11** years for Riaz's income to reach Rs. 14,000.

```json
{Final Answer: "11"}
```

   \[

   \]

   \[

   78 = 24 + (n-1)(-3)
   \]

   54 = (n-1)(-3) \\
   \frac{54}{-3} = n - 1 \\
   -18 = n - 1 \\
   n = -17
   \]

Since \(n\) cannot be negative, there is no term in the given arithmetic progression that equals **78**.

\boxed{\text{There is no such term.}}

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the nth term formula for AP:**  
   The nth term of an Arithmetic Progression (AP) is given by:
   \[
   a_n = a + (n - 1)d
   \]
   where \(a\) is the first term and \(d\) is the common difference.

2. **Express the 20th and 34th terms using the formula:**  
   - For the 20th term (\(n = 20\)):
     \[
     a_{20} = a + (20 - 1)d = a + 19d
     \]
   - For the 34th term (\(n = 34\)):
     \[
     a_{34} = a + (34 - 1)d = a + 33d
     \]

3. **Set up the equation based on the given condition:**  
   According to the problem, after 34 terms, the term exceeds the 20th term by 14:
   \[
   a_{34} - a_{20} = 14
   \]
   Substitute the expressions for \(a_{20}\) and \(a_{34}\):
   \[
   (a + 33d) - (a + 19d) = 14
   \]

4. **Simplify the equation to find \(d\):**  
   Combine like terms:
   \[
   a - a + 33d - 19d = 14 \\
   14d = 14
   \]
   Divide both sides by 14:
   \[
   d = 1
   \]

5. **Verification:**  
   Plugging \(d = 1\) back into the terms:
   - \(a_{20} = a + 19(1) = a + 19\)
   - \(a_{34} = a + 33(1) = a + 33\)
   The difference is \((a + 33) - (a + 19) = 14\), which matches the given condition.

**Final Answer:**
The common difference (\(d\)) in the AP is **1**.

   - They satisfy the AM-GM inequality condition: \(a + b = 12\sqrt{ab}\).

2. **Express in Terms of a Common Variable:**
   Let’s set \(k\) as a positive real number such that:
   \[
   a = (6 + \sqrt{35})k \\
   b = (6 - \sqrt{35})k
   \]
   
3. **Sum and Product:**
   Calculate the sum and product of \(a\) and \(b\):
   \[
   a + b = [(6 + \sqrt{35})k] + [(6 - \sqrt{35})k] = 12k \\
   ab = (6 + \sqrt{35})k \times (6 - \sqrt{35})k = [36 - (\sqrt{35})^2]k^2 = k^2
   \]

   \[

   12k = 12|k|
   \]
   Since \(k\) is positive, this simplifies to:
   \[
   k = 1
   \]

5. **Determine \(a\) and \(b\):**
   Substitute \(k = 1\) back into the expressions for \(a\) and \(b\):
   \[
   a = 6 + \sqrt{35} \\
   b = 6 - \sqrt{35}
   \]
   
6. **Verify the Conditions:**
   - Ratio:
     \[
     \frac{a}{b} = \frac{6 + \sqrt{35}}{6 - \sqrt{35}}
     \]
   - Sum and Product Check:
     \[
     a + b = 12 \\
     ab = (6 + \sqrt{35})(6 - \sqrt{35}) = 36 - 35 = 1
     \]
     Thus, \(a + b = 12\sqrt{ab}\) simplifies to \(12 = 12 \times 1\), which holds true.

ln(1.2) ≈ 0.1823
ln(1.25)≈ 0.2231
ln(1.3)=approx 0.2624

Wait, perhaps I can interpolate between these.

But since our value is 1.256375, which is just a bit above 1.25.

Alternatively, maybe it's better to use logarithms base 10 or another method.

Wait, maybe using the rule of 72 isn't helpful here. Alternatively, perhaps I should accept that without a calculator, approximation might be time-consuming, so let me think about alternative approaches.

Alternatively, since we're dealing with small percentages over three years, the average annual growth rate can be approximated by considering each year's growth and averaging them geometrically.

Wait, but maybe another way: Let's consider x as the average annual growth rate. Then:

(1 + 0.15) * (1 + 0.15) * (1 - 0.05) = (1.15)^2 * 0.95 = ?

As we calculated before, that gives us approximately 1.3225 * 0.95 ≈ 1.256375.

Now, taking the cube root of this:

x^3 = 1.256375

We can write x = (1.256375)^(1/3)

Let me try to compute this using logarithms more accurately.

Taking natural log on both sides:

ln(x) = (1/3)*ln(1.256375)

First, compute ln(1.256375):

I can use the Taylor series for ln around 1: ln(1 + a) ≈ a - a^2/2 + a^3/3 - ..., where a =0.256375.

So:

ln(1.256375)≈ 0.256375 - (0.256375)^2 /2 + (0.256375)^3/3

Compute each term:

First term: 0.256375
Second term: (0.256375^2)/2 = approx (0.06574189)/2 ≈0.032870945
Third term: (0.256375^3)/3≈(0.01689495)/3≈0.00563165

So, ln(1.256375) ≈ 0.256375 - 0.032870945 + 0.00563165 ≈ ?

Calculate step by step:

0.256375 - 0.032870945 = 0.223504055

Then, add 0.00563165: 0.223504055 + 0.00563165 ≈ 0.229135705

So ln(1.256375)≈0.2291357

Then, multiply by (1/3):

ln(x)= (1/3)*0.2291357 ≈0.076378567

Now, compute x = e^(0.076378567)

We can use the Taylor series for e^y around y=0:

e^y≈1 + y + y^2/2! + y^3/3! +...

With y=0.076378567

Compute up to, say, four terms.

First term: 1
Second term: 0.076378567 ≈ 0.076379
Third term: (0.076378567)^2 / 2 ≈ (0.005831) /2≈0.0029155
Fourth term: (0.076378567)^3 /6≈(0.000446)/6≈0.0000743

So summing these:

1 + 0.076379 =1.076379

+0.0029155=1.0792945

+0.0000743≈1.0793688

Higher order terms would be negligible.

So x ≈1.0793688, so the average annual growth rate is approximately 7.93688%.

Wait, but let me check that again because earlier I thought it was around 1.078, which is about 7.8%. But this approximation gives us ~7.94%, close to our initial guess.

But wait, perhaps the linear approximation is better here. Let's see: We know that (1.08)^3≈1.2597, and we have ln(1.08)= approx 0.0770

Wait, no, because 1.08^3=1.2597 as before.

So our value of x is (1.256375)^(1/3), which is less than 1.08 by a small amount.

Alternatively, perhaps we can use linear approximation around y=1.08:

Let me write f(y)=y^3

We have f(1.08)=1.2597

We need to find x such that f(x)=1.256375, which is 1.2597 - 0.003325.

So the change in y from 1.08 would be delta_y≈delta_f/(3*(1.08)^2)

Since f'(y)=3y^2

At y=1.08, f'(y)=3*(1.08)^2 ≈3*1.1664≈3.4992

So delta_f = -0.003325

Thus, delta_y ≈ (-0.003325)/3.4992≈-0.000947

Therefore, x= y + delta_y≈1.08 - 0.000947≈1.079053

So approximates to ~1.07905 or 7.905% annual growth rate.

Hmm, that's close to our earlier approximation of about 7.936%.

Given the slight discrepancy due to approximation methods, perhaps it's safe to say around 7.94%.

Alternatively, maybe a better approach is required here, but since this is getting complicated without exact calculations, I'll proceed with the approximate value.

The average annual growth rate over three years can be found by taking the geometric mean of the growth factors each year.

Each year's growth factor: 1.15 (for +15%), then next year it's not given directly but perhaps assuming same? Wait no, wait—the problem statement says "the average rate," so maybe we should compute the overall return and then find the constant growth rate that would yield the same result.

Wait, actually, in this case, since only one year is explicitly stated with a 15% increase, and nothing else, perhaps the question expects us to assume that after that year, it's stable or something? Wait no—wait, maybe I misread the problem earlier. Let me check.

Wait no—that's not correct either because in my initial thoughts I thought perhaps another factor comes into play. Wait—it's possible that maybe after the first year's growth, there's some constant rate for the next two years to achieve an average return.

"SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS/SYS ### Given:
- **Numbers**: 9, 8, 10, \( K \), 12
- **Arithmetic Mean (AM)**: 9
- **Geometric Mean (GM)**: 8

### Step 1: Understanding the Concepts
1. **Arithmetic Mean (AM)**:
   \[
   \text{AM} = \frac{\text{Sum of all terms}}{\text{Number of terms}}
   \]
   
2. **Geometric Mean (GM)**:
   \[
   \text{GM} = \sqrt[\text{n}]{\text{Product of all terms}}
   \]

Given that there are 5 numbers in total:

\[
9 = \frac{\text{Sum}}{5}
\]
\[
\text{Sum} = 9 \times 5 = 45
\]

\[

\[
\text{Product} = 8^5 = 32768
\]

So, the product of all terms is **32768**.

\[

First, I recognize that this is a linear nonhomogeneous recurrence relation.

The general solution for such equations involves finding both the homogeneous and particular solutions.

For the homogeneous part, an = 5an-1, the characteristic equation r = 5 gives us the homogeneous solution as An = C * 5^n.

Next, I'll find the particular solution. Since the nonhomogeneous term is a constant (5), I assume the particular solution is also a constant, D.

Substituting D into the recurrence relation: D = 5D + 5.
Solving for D gives me D = -5/4.

Combining both parts, the general solution becomes An = C * 5^n - 5/4.

Using the initial condition A1 = 5 to determine the constant C:
5 = C * 5^1 - 5/4 ⇒ 25C - 5/4 = 5 ⇒ 25C = 25/4 ⇒ C = 1/4.

Therefore, the final solution is An = (1/4) * 5^n - 5/4.

To solve the recurrence relation \( a_n = 5a_{n-1} + 5 \) with the initial condition \( a_1 = 5 \), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Find the Homogeneous Solution
The homogeneous part of the recurrence is:
\[ a_n^{(h)} = 5a_{n-1}^{(h)} \]

This can be solved by finding the characteristic equation:
\[ r = 5 \]

So, the homogeneous solution is:
\[ a_n^{(h)} = C \cdot 5^n \]
where \( C \) is a constant.

### Step 2: Find the Particular Solution
Since the nonhomogeneous term is a constant (5), we assume a particular solution of the form:
\[ a_n^{(p)} = D \]

Substitute \( a_n^{(p)} \) into the recurrence relation:
\[
D = 5D + 5 \\
-4D = 5 \\
D = -\frac{5}{4}
\]

### Step 3: Combine Homogeneous and Particular Solutions
The general solution is:
\[ a_n = a_n^{(h)} + a_n^{(p)} \]
\[ a_n = C \cdot 5^n - \frac{5}{4} \]

### Step 4: Apply the Initial Condition to Find \( C \)
Use \( a_1 = 5 \):
\[
5 = C \cdot 5^1 - \frac{5}{4} \\
5 = 5C - \frac{5}{4} \\
5 + \frac{5}{4} = 5C \\
\frac{25}{4} = 5C \\
C = \frac{5}{4}
\]

### Step 5: Write the Final Solution
Substitute \( C \) back into the general solution:
\[ a_n = \frac{5}{4} \cdot 5^n - \frac{5}{4} \]
\[ a_n = \frac{1}{4} \left(5^{n+1} - 5\right) \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{\frac{1}{4}\left(5^{n+1}-5\right)}
\]

To solve the given geometric series problem, let's break it down step by step.

### Given Series:
\[ 3 + \frac{6}{3} + \frac{12}{9} + \dots \]

### Step 1: Identify the Type of Sequence
First, observe the pattern in the series to determine if it is a **Geometric Progression (GP)**. In a GP, each term after the first is obtained by multiplying the previous term by a constant called the common ratio (\( r \)).

Let's calculate the ratio between consecutive terms:
- Second term divided by the first term:
  \[
  \frac{\frac{6}{3}}{3} = \frac{2}{3}
  \]
  
- Third term divided by the second term:
  \[
  \frac{\frac{12}{9}}{\frac{6}{3}} = \frac{\frac{4}{3}}{2} = \frac{2}{3}
  \]

Since the ratio is consistent, this confirms it's a **GP** with:
- First term (\( a \)) = \( 3 \)
- Common ratio (\( r \)) = \( \frac{2}{3} \)

### Step 2: Calculate the Sum of the First 5 Terms
The formula for the sum of the first \( n \) terms in a GP is:
\[
S_n = a \times \frac{1 - r^n}{1 - r}
\]

Plugging in the values to find the sum of the first 5 terms (\( S_5 \)):
\[
S_5 = 3 \times \frac{1 - \left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^5}{1 - \frac{2}{3}}
\]
\[
S_5 = 3 \times \frac{1 - \frac{32}{243}}{\frac{1}{3}}
\]
\[
S_5 = 3 \times \frac{\frac{211}{243}}{\frac{1}{3}}
\]
\[
S_5 = 3 \times \frac{633}{243}
\]
\[
S_5 \approx 7.96296

a3 = a2 * r = 8*8 = 64

The problem requires finding the number of terms \( n \) such that the sum of the first \( n \) terms of a geometric progression (GP) with first term \( a = 1 \) and common ratio \( r = 4 \) equals 63. The formula for the sum of the first \( n \) terms is:

\[ S_n = \frac{a(r^n - 1)}{r - 1} \]

Substituting the given values:

\[ 63 = \frac{4^n - 1}{3} \]
\[ 4^n - 1 = 189 \]
\[ 4^n = 190 \]

Taking logarithms to solve for \( n \):

\[ n = \log_4(190) \approx 3.756 \]

Since the number of terms must be an integer, there is no solution with \( a = 1 \), \( r = 4 \), and \( S_n = 63 \).

Answer: There is no such integer number of terms \( n \) that satisfies the given conditions.

However, if we consider non-integer terms (which isn't typical in GPs), \( n \approx 3.756 \). But since this isn't a valid term count, the answer remains there's no solution with an integer \( n \).

But wait, perhaps I made a mistake earlier by miscalculating the sum for certain \( n \) values.

Let me double-check:

- For \( n = 3 \): Sum is \( (4^3 - 1)/3 = (64 - 1)/3 = 21 \)
- For \( n = 4 \): Sum is \( (4^4 - 1)/3 = (256 - 1)/3 = 85 \)

So, between \( n = 3 \) and \( n = 4 \), the sum goes from 21 to 85. Since 63 lies in this range but requires a non-integer \( n \), which isn't allowed.

Therefore, confirming that no integer solution exists.

Answer: There is no such integer number of terms \( n \) that satisfies \( S_n = 63 \).

However, the problem states "how many terms," implying it expects an answer. Therefore, based on calculations:

The sum equals exactly 63 when \( n \approx 3.756 \), which is not possible in a GP as term counts are integers.

Thus, there's no solution with integer \( n \).

Answer: There is no such number of terms; an exact solution doesn't exist with integer values.

The problem involves finding the common ratio \( r \) of a geometric progression (GP) where certain conditions related to sums are given. After considering different possibilities and assumptions about whether the GP is finite or infinite, as well as how the "five" factor applies to the sum of terms, it was deduced that for an infinite GP, setting up the equation based on the ratio of sums leads to a straightforward solution.

For an infinite GP with first term \( a \) and common ratio \( r \), where the sum of all odd positioned terms is five times the sum of even positioned terms:

\[
S_{\text{odd}} = a + ar^2 + ar^4 + \dots = \frac{a}{1 - r^2}
\]
\[
S_{\text{even}} = ar + ar^3 + ar^5 + \dots = \frac{ar}{1 - r^2}
\]

Setting \( S_{\text{odd}} = 5 \times S_{\text{even}} \):

\[
\frac{a}{1 - r^2} = 5 \times \frac{ar}{1 - r^2}
\]

Simplifying, we find:

\[
1 = 5r \implies r = \frac{1}{5}
\]

Thus, the common ratio is:

\[
\boxed{\dfrac{1}{5}}
\]

**Final Answer**

To determine the annual payment required to clear a loan of ₹12,000 at an annual compound interest rate of 24%, we use the present value formula for an annuity. The formula to calculate the payment (PMT) is:

\[
PMT = \frac{PV \times r}{1 - (1 + r)^{-n}}
\]

Where:
- \( PV \) is the present value (₹12,000)
- \( r \) is the annual interest rate (24% or 0.24)
- \( n \) is the number of periods (2 years)

Plugging in the values:

\[
PMT = \frac{12000 \times 0.24}{1 - (1 + 0.24)^{-2}}
\]

First, calculate the denominator:
\[
(1 + 0.24)^2 = 1.5376
\]
\[
(1 + 0.24)^{-2} = \frac{1}{1.5376} \approx 0.6503
\]
\[
1 - 0.6503 = 0.3497
\]

Next, calculate the numerator:
\[
12000 \times 0.24 = 2880
\]

Now, divide the numerator by the denominator:
\[
PMT = \frac{2880}{0.3497} \approx 8236
\]

Thus, the annual payment required is approximately ₹8236.

\[
\boxed{8236}
\]

First, I need to determine the total cost of the scooter. The user mentioned that Shamsad Ali purchased a scooter worth Rs. 44,000 and paid Rs. 8,000 in cash.

So, the remaining amount to be financed is:
Total Cost - Cash Payment = Rs. 44,000 - Rs. 8,000 = Rs. 36,000

Next, there's an additional interest of 20% on this remaining amount. To calculate the interest:
Interest = 20% of Rs. 36,000 = 0.20 * 36,000 = Rs. 7,200

Now, I'll add this interest to the principal amount to find the total installment amount that needs to be paid in equal annual installments over a year:
Total Installment Amount = Principal + Interest = Rs. 36,000 + Rs. 7,200 = Rs. 43,200

Since the interest is being split into two equal installments, each installment will be half of the total interest plus their share of the principal.

Calculating each installment:
Each Installment = (Total Installment Amount / Number of Installments) + (Interest / Number of Installments)
= (Rs. 43,200 / 2) + (Rs. 7,200 / 2)
= Rs. 21,600 + Rs. 3,600
= Rs. 25,200

Therefore, the monthly installment amount is Rs. 25,200.
</think>

To determine the monthly installment amount Shamsad Ali has to pay for the scooter, follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Amount to be Financed:**
   
   Total Cost of Scooter = Rs. 44,000
   
   Cash Payment = Rs. 8,000
   
   Amount to be Financed = Total Cost - Cash Payment
   \[
   \text{Amount to be Financed} = 44,000 - 8,000 = 36,000 \text{ INR}
   \]

2. **Calculate the Interest on the Amount:**
   
   Rate of Interest (\(r\)) = 20\% per annum
   
   Since the interest is to be paid over one year and split into two equal installments, we'll calculate half-yearly interest.
   
   Half-yearly Interest Rate = \( \frac{20\%}{2} = 10\% \)
   
   Interest for Each Installment:
   \[
   \text{Interest per installment} = 36,000 \times \frac{10\%}{2} = 36,000 \times 5\% = 1,800 \text{ INR}
   \]
   
   However, the correct approach is to calculate the annual interest and split it into two equal parts:
   
   Annual Interest (\(I\)) = \( 36,000 \times 20\% = 7,200 \text{ INR} \)
   
   Each Installment's Interest Portion:
   \[
   \frac{I}{2} = \frac{7,200}{2} = 3,600 \text{ INR}
   \]

3. **Calculate the Principal Amount for Each Installment:**
   
   Since the total amount to be financed is Rs. 36,000 and it's split into two equal installments:
   \[
   \text{Principal per installment} = \frac{36,000}{2} = 18,000 \text{ INR}
   \]

4. **Determine the Total Installment Amount:**
   
   Each installment consists of the principal plus its share of interest:
   \[
   \text{Installment per period} = 18,000 + 3,600 = 21,600 \text{ INR}
   \]

**Final Answer:**  
Each installment Shamsad Ali has to pay is **\boxed{21,600 \text{ INR}}**.

Alright, let me try to figure out this problem. So, a person deposited 10,000 rupees in a bank that gives a simple interest rate of 5% annually. We need to find out how much money will be there after 15 years and then also calculate the total amount after 20 years.

First, I remember that simple interest is calculated using the formula: 

Interest (I) = Principal (P) × Rate (R) × Time (T)

Since it's simple interest, the interest doesn't compound; it's just a fixed percentage each year on the original principal. So for each year, the interest would be 10,000 × 5% which is 500 rupees.

For 15 years, the total interest would be 500 × 15 = 7,500 rupees. Adding that to the principal gives us the total amount after 15 years: 10,000 + 7,500 = 17,500 rupees.

Now, for 20 years, the interest would be 500 × 20 = 10,000 rupees. Adding that to the principal gives us 10,000 + 10,000 = 20,000 rupees after 20 years.

Wait a second, I'm just using simple interest here because it's straightforward and doesn't require considering any compounding effects. So these calculations should be correct based on the given information.
</think>

To determine the total amount in the bank after a certain number of years with a simple interest rate, we can use the formula for simple interest:

\[ A = P + (P \times R \times T) \]

Where:
- \( A \) is the total amount
- \( P \) is the principal amount (10,000 rupees)
- \( R \) is the annual interest rate (5% or 0.05)
- \( T \) is the time in years

### After 15 Years:
\[ A = 10000 + (10000 \times 0.05 \times 15) \]
\[ A = 10000 + (7500) \]
\[ A = 17,500 \text{ rupees} \]

### After 20 Years:
\[ A = 10000 + (10000 \times 0.05 \times 20) \]
\[ A = 10000 + (10000) \]
\[ A = 20,000 \text{ rupees} \]

**Final Answer:**
- Amount after 15 years: "17500" rupees
- Total amount after 20 years: "20000" rupees

**Final Answer**

Given that 30 men can finish a job in 14 days, we know the total work \( W \) is calculated as follows:

\[ W = 30 \text{ men} \times 14 \text{ days} = 420 \text{ man-days} \]

We need to determine how many days (\( d \)) it would take for a group of workers to complete the same job if they are joined by 5 more men after \( d \) days. The total work done can be expressed as:

\[ x \times d + (x + 5) \times (8 - d) = 420 \]

where \( x \) is the initial number of workers. Simplifying this equation, we get:

\[ xd + (x + 5)(8 - d) = 420 \]
\[ xd + (x(8 - d) + 5(8 - d)) = 420 \]
\[ xd + 8x - xd + 40 - 5d = 420 \]
\[ 8x - 5d + 40 = 420 \]
\[ 8x - 5d = 380 \]

We solve for \( d \):

\[ 5d = 8x - 380 \]
\[ d = \frac{8x - 380}{5} \]

Since \( d \) must be an integer, \( 8x - 380 \) must be divisible by 5. This implies:

\[ 8x \equiv 380 \pmod{5} \]
\[ 8 \equiv 3 \pmod{5} \]
\[ 3x \equiv 0 \pmod{5} \]

Thus, \( x \) must be a multiple of 5. Let \( x = 5k \):

\[ d = \frac{40k - 380}{5} \]
\[ d = 8k - 76 \]

For \( d \) to be positive:

\[ 8k - 76 > 0 \]
\[ 8k > 76 \]
\[ k > 9.5 \]

The smallest integer \( k \) is 10, giving:

\[ x = 5 \times 10 = 50 \]
\[ d = 8 \times 10 - 76 = 4 \]

Thus, the value of \( d \) is \boxed{4}.

To determine if **330** is an element of the arithmetic progression (sequence) defined by **5, 11, 17, 23,...**, we can follow these steps:

### Step 1: Identify the First Term and Common Difference
- **First term (\(a_1\))**: \(5\)
- **Common difference (\(d\))**: The difference between consecutive terms:
  \[
  d = 11 - 5 = 6
  \]

### Step 2: Use the Formula for the nth Term of an Arithmetic Progression (AP)
The general formula to find the \(n^{th}\) term (\(a_n\)) in an AP is:
\[
a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
\]
We want to check if **330** can be expressed as such a term.

### Step 3: Set Up the Equation and Solve for \(n\)
Set \(a_n = 330\) and plug in the known values:
\[
330 = 5 + (n - 1) \times 6
\]
Subtract **5** from both sides to isolate the term with \(n\):
\[
330 - 5 = (n - 1) \times 6 \\
325 = (n - 1) \times 6
\]
Divide both sides by **6** to solve for \(n - 1\):
\[
\frac{325}{6} = n - 1
\]
Calculate the division:
\[
n - 1 \approx 54.1667
\]
Add **1** to both sides to find \(n\):
\[
n \approx 55.1667
\]

### Step 4: Analyze the Result
The value of \(n\) is approximately **55.1667**, which is not an integer.

In an arithmetic progression, \(n\) must be a positive integer because it represents the term number in the sequence. Since **330** does not correspond to an integer value of \(n\), it is not part of this particular AP.

Now, the question is about whether these valuations form an arithmetic progression (AP). In an AP, each term increases by a constant difference. So, I need to check if the amounts at the end of each year form such a sequence.

So for each subsequent year, the amount increases by PR/100. That means the difference between consecutive terms is constant, which is a characteristic of an AP. Therefore, yes, these valuations do form an arithmetic progression with a common difference of PR/100.

Now, to find out how much 30 years later (i.e., in 2030) the investment will be worth, I can use the formula for the nth term of an AP. The first term a1 is P(1 + R/100), and each subsequent term increases by PR/100.

So the amount in 2030 would be 5.8 times the principal invested in 2000.

- Amount (A) = P + (P * R * T)/100 = P(1 + RT/100)

To determine the number of rows (or rows) in the flower bed, we can use the concept of an **Arithmetic Progression (AP)**. 

### Given:
- **First term (\(a_1\))**: 46 petals
- **Last term (\(a_n\))**: 4 petals
- **Common difference (\(d\))**: -2 petals per row

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Understand the Formula for the nth Term of an AP**:
   
   The formula to find the \(n^{th}\) term of an arithmetic progression is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   
2. **Plug in the Known Values**:
   
   We know that \(a_n = 4\), \(a_1 = 46\), and \(d = -2\). Substitute these into the formula:
   \[
   4 = 46 + (n - 1)(-2)
   \]
   
3. **Solve for \(n\)**:

   \[
   4 = 46 - 2(n - 1) \\
   4 = 46 - 2n + 2 \\
   4 = 48 - 2n \\
   -44 = -2n \\
   n = \frac{44}{2} \\
   n = 22
   \]
   
### **Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{22}

a6 = a + (6-1)d
24 = a + 5d

The problem involves finding the 58th term of an arithmetic progression with 100 terms, where the 6th term is 24 and the last term (100th) is 212. 

**Solution:**
1. **Set up equations based on the given information:**
   - The nth term formula for an AP is \( a_n = a + (n-1)d \).
   - For the 6th term (\( n=6 \)): \( a + 5d = 24 \) ...(Equation 1)
   - For the 100th term (\( n=100 \)): \( a + 99d = 212 \) ...(Equation 2)

2. **Subtract Equation 1 from Equation 2 to find \( d \):**
   - \( (a + 99d) - (a + 5d) = 212 - 24 \)
   - Simplifies to: \( 94d = 188 \) → \( d = 2 \)

   - \( a + 5(2) = 24 \)
   - Simplifies to: \( a = 14 \)

   - \( a_{58} = 14 + (58-1)(2) = 14 + 114 = 128 \)

The 58th term is **128**.

Hmm, I think this is an arithmetic progression problem because the amount she owes increases by a fixed amount each year. The principal is Rs.10,000 in 1995, and every subsequent year, she pays an additional Rs.400 as interest. So, her total debt forms an AP where each term represents her debt at the end of each year.

Let me recall the formula for the nth term of an arithmetic progression: a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d. Here, a_1 is the first term, d is the common difference, and n is the number of terms.

In this case, the first term a_1 would be Rs.10,000 because that's the initial loan amount in 1995. The common difference d is Rs.400 since that's how much she pays each year as interest. We need to find n when a_n becomes Rs.14,000.

Plugging into the formula: 14,000 = 10,000 + (n - 1)*400.

Solving for n:

Subtract 10,000 from both sides: 4,000 = (n - 1)*400

Divide both sides by 400: 10 = n - 1

</think>

The total amount owed by Sabita increases by Rs.400 each year due to interest. Starting from Rs.10,000 in 1995, we can model her debt using an arithmetic progression. We need to find the number of years \( n \) it takes for her debt to reach Rs.14,000.

The formula for the nth term of an arithmetic sequence is:
\[
a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
\]
where:
- \( a_1 = 10,000 \)
- \( d = 400 \)
- \( a_n = 14,000 \)

Substituting the values into the formula:
\[
14,000 = 10,000 + (n - 1) \times 400
\]

Solving for \( n \):
\[
14,000 - 10,000 = (n - 1) \times 400 \\
4,000 = (n - 1) \times 400 \\
\frac{4,000}{400} = n - 1 \\
10 = n - 1 \\
n = 11
\]

After 11 years from 1995, the year is \( 1995 + 11 = 2006 \).

To solve the problem where an amount S is paid back in installments with each subsequent payment being Rs. 40 less than the previous one, we can model this as an arithmetic series.

1. **Identify Variables:**
   - Let the number of installments be \( n \).
   - The first installment is \( a \).
   - The common difference (\( d \)) between consecutive payments is \( -40 \) (since each installment decreases by Rs. 40).

2. **Sum of the Arithmetic Series:**
   The total sum \( S \) paid back can be calculated using the formula:
   \[
   S = \frac{n}{2} \times [2a + (n - 1)d]
   \]
   Substituting the known values:
   \[
   1400 = \frac{n}{2} \times [2a - 40(n - 1)]
   \]

3. **Solve for \( a \):**
   Simplify the equation to express \( a \) in terms of \( n \):
   \[
   1400 = \frac{n}{2} \times (2a - 40n + 40)
   \]
   Multiply both sides by 2:
   \[
   2800 = n(2a - 40n + 40)
   \]
   Divide both sides by \( n \):
   \[
   2a - 40n + 40 = \frac{2800}{n}
   \]
   Rearranging terms:
   \[
   a = \frac{1400}{n} + 20n - 20
   \]

4. **Determine Possible Installment Counts (\( n \)):**
   Since \( a \) must be positive, we can find possible values of \( n \) that make \( a \) an integer.

   For example:
   - If \( n = 5 \):
     \[
     a = \frac{1400}{5} + 20(5) - 20 = 280 + 100 - 20 = 360
     \]
     Installments: Rs. 360, 320, 280, 240, 200.

   - If \( n = 7 \):
     \[
     a = \frac{1400}{7} + 20(7) - 20 = 200 + 140 - 20 = 320
     \]
     Installments: Rs. 320, 280, 240, 200, 160, 120, 80.

5. **Conclusion:**
   The exact amounts of each installment depend on the number of installments (\( n \)). Without specifying \( n \), we can't determine unique individual amounts. However, for specific values of \( n \), we can calculate the corresponding payments using the formula derived above.

**Final Answer:**

The first payment is Rs. 360 when there are 5 installments (resulting in payments of Rs. 360, 320, 280, 240, and 200). For other values of \( n \), similar calculations can be performed to find the respective installment amounts.

First, I identify that this problem involves an arithmetic progression where we're given specific information about certain terms and their relationship.

Let me denote the first term as \( a_1 \) and the common difference as \( d \). The general formula for the \( n \)-th term of an arithmetic sequence is:
\[ a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d \]

According to the problem, the 34th term exceeds the 20th term by 14. Setting up the equation based on this information:

\[ a_{34} - a_{20} = 14 \]
Substituting the formula for each term:
\[ (a_1 + 33d) - (a_1 + 19d) = 14 \]

Simplifying the equation by canceling out \( a_1 \):
\[ 14d = 14 \]

Finally, solving for \( d \) gives us:
\[ d = 1 \]
</think>

To determine the **common difference** (\( d \)) in the given arithmetic progression (A.P.), let's follow these steps:

### Given Information:
- The 34th term exceeds the 20th term by 14.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Understand the Formula for the \( n^{th} \) Term of an A.P.:**
   
   The general formula to find the \( n^{th} \) term (\( a_n \)) in an arithmetic progression is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   where:
   - \( a_1 \) is the first term,
   - \( d \) is the common difference,
   - \( n \) is the term number.

2. **Express the Given Terms Using the Formula:**
   
   - For the **34th term** (\( a_{34} \)):
     \[
     a_{34} = a_1 + (34 - 1)d = a_1 + 33d
     \]
     
   - For the **20th term** (\( a_{20} \)):
     \[
     a_{20} = a_1 + (20 - 1)d = a_1 + 19d
     \]

3. **Set Up the Equation Based on the Given Condition:**
   
   According to the problem, the difference between the 34th term and the 20th term is 14:
   \[
   a_{34} - a_{20} = 14
   \]
   
   Substitute the expressions from step 2:
   \[
   (a_1 + 33d) - (a_1 + 19d) = 14
   \]

4. **Simplify the Equation:**
   
   Simplify by canceling out \( a_1 \):
   \[
   33d - 19d = 14 \\
   14d = 14
   \]
   
5. **Solve for \( d \):**
   
   Divide both sides of the equation by 14:
   \[
   d = \frac{14}{14} \\
   d = 1
   \]

### Final Answer:

\[
\boxed{1}
\]

The arithmetic mean (AM) of numbers A = 10 and B = 8 is calculated by adding them together and dividing by 2:

\[ \text{AM} = \frac{10 + 8}{2} = \frac{18}{2} = 9 \]

The geometric mean (GM) is found by taking the square root of their product:

\[ \text{GM} = \sqrt{10 \times 8} = \sqrt{80} = 4\sqrt{5} \approx 8.944 \]

Thus, both means are calculated as shown.

First, I need to define the two numbers. Let’s call them \( a = 6 + \sqrt{35} \) and \( b = 6 - \sqrt{35} \).

Next, I calculate their sum:
\[
a + b = (6 + \sqrt{35}) + (6 - \sqrt{35}) = 12
\]

Then, I find the arithmetic mean by dividing the sum by 2:
\[
AM = \frac{a + b}{2} = \frac{12}{2} = 6
\]

Now, I calculate the geometric mean using the formula \( GM = \sqrt{ab} \):
\[
ab = (6 + \sqrt{35})(6 - \sqrt{35}) = 36 - (\sqrt{35})^2 = 36 - 35 = 1
\]
\[
GM = \sqrt{1} = 1
\]

Therefore, the arithmetic mean is 6 and the geometric mean is 1.

Let's solve the problem step by step.

**Given:**
- The two numbers are \( a = 6 + \sqrt{35} \) and \( b = 6 - \sqrt{35} \).
- We need to find their Arithmetic Mean (AM) and Geometric Mean (GM).

**Step 1: Calculate the Sum of the Numbers**

\[
a + b = (6 + \sqrt{35}) + (6 - \sqrt{35})
\]

Simplify:

\[
a + b = 12
\]

**Step 2: Find the Arithmetic Mean (AM)**

The formula for the Arithmetic Mean is:

\[
AM = \frac{a + b}{2}
\]

Substitute the sum calculated:

\[
AM = \frac{12}{2} = 6
\]

So, **Arithmetic Mean (AM) = 6**.

---

**Step 3: Calculate the Product of the Numbers**

The formula for the Geometric Mean is based on the product \( ab \):

\[
ab = (6 + \sqrt{35})(6 - \sqrt{35})
\]

Use the difference of squares formula:

\[
( a + b )( a - b ) = a^2 - b^2
\]

Here, \( a = 6 \) and \( b = \sqrt{35} \):

\[
ab = 6^2 - (\sqrt{35})^2 = 36 - 35 = 1
\]

**Step 4: Find the Geometric Mean (GM)**

The formula for the Geometric Mean is:

\[
GM = \sqrt{ab}
\]

Substitute the product calculated:

\[
GM = \sqrt{1} = 1
\]

So, **Geometric Mean (GM) = 1**.

---

**Final Answer:**

\[
\boxed{\text{Arithmetic Mean} = 6,\quad \text{Geometric Mean} = 1}
\]

The original total sum with the mistake is \(40 \times 38 = 1520\). The incorrect number was recorded as 36 instead of 56, which means the sum was understated by \(56 - 36 = 20\). Adding this difference back to the original total gives the correct sum: \(1520 + 20 = 1540\). The correct arithmetic mean is then \(\frac{1540}{40} = 38.5\).

**Final Answer:**
